# Paper candidates for robot-laboratory tasks

Last screened: **2026-10-22**. Candidate count: **296**.

This is a lightweight discovery list of papers that may support future robot-laboratory task design. Every entry is a **CANDIDATE**, not a fully read source, reviewed task design, converted task, runnable experiment or asset package. Candidates do not change the repository's reviewed-design or task-package counts.

Scope: experimental papers in *Nature*, journals whose full titles begin with *Nature*, or *Science*. **npj journals are excluded.** This batch focuses on nonbiological mechanics, materials, fabrication and measurement.

## Candidate titles

| Paper and official source | Journal / year | DOI | Preliminary task suitability | Source access checked |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Percolation transition in entangled granular networks](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-025-66228-3) | Nature Communications / 2025 | 10.1038/s41467-025-66228-3 | Repeated particle preparation and reset, vibration, cluster lifting and weighing suggest a manipulation-and-measurement task | HTML + selected Methods accessible; OA |
| [From creep to flow: Granular materials under cyclic shear](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-48176-6) | Nature Communications / 2024 | 10.1038/s41467-024-48176-6 | Comparing particle surfaces, shear histories and repeated tomography suggests a sample-tracking and instrument-coordination task | HTML + selected Methods accessible; OA |
| [High-throughput fabrication of soft magneto-origami machines](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-022-31900-5) | Nature Communications / 2022 | 10.1038/s41467-022-31900-5 | Sheet fabrication, folding, magnetic programming and actuation comparisons suggest a multi-equipment fabrication-and-testing task | HTML + selected Methods accessible; OA |
| [Spatially programmable origami networks enable high-density mechanical computing for autonomous robotics](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-025-64956-0) | Nature Communications / 2025 | 10.1038/s41467-025-64956-0 | Film folding, switch and circuit assembly, mechanical measurements and logic tests suggest a staged assembly-and-verification task | HTML + selected Methods accessible; OA |
| [Polymer-inspired mechanical metamaterials](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-77199-4) | Nature Communications / 2026 | 10.1038/s41467-026-77199-4 | Printed synthetic-resin specimen preparation, mechanical testing and image-based strain measurements suggest a materials-testing task | HTML + selected Methods accessible; OA |
| [The sands of time run faster near the end](https://www.nature.com/articles/ncomms15551) | Nature Communications / 2017 | 10.1038/ncomms15551 | Hopper assembly, grain loading, air-versus-submerged comparisons and weighing/imaging suggest a multi-condition laboratory task | HTML + selected Methods accessible; OA |
| [Anomalous flocking in nonpolar granular Brownian vibrators](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-50479-7) | Nature Communications / 2024 | 10.1038/s41467-024-50479-7 | Particle preparation, platform leveling, density-dependent loading and extended vibration/video measurements suggest a setup-and-observation task | HTML + selected Methods accessible; OA |

## Screening and access limits

- All seven linked official publisher pages were opened on the screening date. Titles, journal names, publication years and DOIs were checked; abstracts and selected Methods passages were screened for concrete experimental work
- **OA** means the publisher labels the article Open Access. Main HTML access does not establish access to every supplement or permission to reuse every source component
- Complete papers, PDFs, supplementary information, movies, code and raw datasets have **not** been fully reviewed for this list. No paper-level completeness or reproducibility claim is made
- Suitability descriptions are preliminary inferences. They identify possible task structure, not extracted operating procedures or evidence that a robot can perform the work
- Exact DOIs and titles were checked against the existing repository and previously accepted task-design inventory to avoid duplicate candidate entries
- The entangled-network and polymer-inspired-metamaterials pages list **CC BY-NC-ND 4.0**. Their inclusion is a citation and discovery record, not permission to adapt or redistribute their source content. Other source-component rights require their own check before reuse

Further source review and task conversion are separate work. This list adds no operating parameters, biological-engineering procedures, 3D assets or experimental execution claims.

| [Topological mechanical metamaterial for robust and ductile one-way fracturing](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-69026-7) | Nature Communications / 2026 | 10.1038/s41467-026-69026-7 | Cut-sheet specimen preparation, pre-test inspection, mechanical loading and polarized imaging suggest a fabrication-and-testing task | Reader HTML; cookie-error marker; OA |

### Screening scope for these five additions

- Exact titles, official journal names, publication years and DOIs were checked against the linked publisher pages on 2026-10-07; abstracts and the selected sections below were screened

- Propagation-adaptive holography: Methods, Experimental details
- Compact light-field photography: Methods, Experimental setup and Camera calibration
- Surface gravity waves: Results, Experimental setup; Methods, Experiments
- One-way fracturing: selected experimental Results; Methods, Experiments
- **Access caveat for every addition:** Publisher HTML readable through web reader; cookie-error marker reported; PDF/supplement access not verified. This is not a claim of clean live access or complete full-text review. No retry or alternate route was used to resolve the marker; papers with actual access denials or unusable returns were excluded
- Complete written sources, supplementary information, movies, code and raw datasets were **not reviewed** for these additions. No PDF, supplement, media or dataset was acquired for this batch
- Suitability remains a preliminary inference, not a robot-feasibility or experimental-reproduction claim. Apparatus, laser, fabrication and mechanical-test safety need separate qualification before any task conversion
- Exact titles and DOIs were deduplicated against the seven existing candidates, current public task sources and the previously accepted task-design inventory
- The first four additions display **CC BY 4.0**; the one-way-fracturing article displays **CC BY-NC-ND 4.0**. These are citations and discovery notes, not permission to adapt or redistribute source content; third-party component rights remain unverified

- Foam imaging: selected physical Results and main material, rheometry, weighing and imaging Methods describe confined foams prepared from oil-in-water emulsions and LED-lit time-lapse acquisition. - Bead-chain mechanics: selected macroscopic Results, experimental Methods and Sections S2.1–S2.4 and S3 plus selected caption text from the thirty-one-page SI describe vibration-driven chains with binding-side, spring-length and baseline comparisons. Suitability is limited to prepared macroscopic components; DNA, ion chemistry and biological extensions are excluded. Some extracted SI equations were garbled and were not validated
- Particle motion: selected experimental Results, Figures 1–2 caption text and main apparatus Methods describe confined vibrobots with different mass configurations, calibrated imaging and vibration checks. Border-collision events are excluded from the analyzed trajectories. Main prose and Figure 2c caption disagree on which direction lags; only an orientation–velocity delay is retained here. Suitability concerns prepared, cured particles and inert mass pieces, excluding printing, solvent washing, UV curing and filing. The linked SI failed on first retrieval and was not read
- Fiber metrology: selected physical Results and timing-calibration Methods, plus selected Notes 1–2 and 5–7 and caption text from the thirteen-page SI, describe prepared-fiber perturbation/reversal, polarization acquisitions and detector-response checks. Mode energies and Stokes estimates are derived; timing-bin spacing is not the achieved detector resolution, and best-pixel sensitivity is not an array-wide claim. Suitability concerns prepared intact fibers and qualified instruments, excluding fiber fabrication/cleaving and biological applications. Low detected photon counts do not establish laser safety

| [When bubbles bounce or stick](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-70921-2) | Nature Communications / 2026 | 10.1038/s41467-026-70921-2 | Prepared-input bubble-impact imaging suggests an instrument-coordination, sample-history and observation-comparison task | Reader HTML; cookie-error marker; OA; main scientific text/Methods and selected experimental SI text read; figure pixels unverified |
| [Bubbles enable volumetric negative compressibility in metastable elastocapillary systems](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-49136-w) | Nature Communications / 2024 | 10.1038/s41467-024-49136-w | Prepared-lamina identity tracking, paired optical/pressure records and repeat-observation comparison suggest a bounded measurement task | Reader HTML; cookie-error marker; OA; selected main text/Methods read; first SI fetch failed, unread and held |

### Screening scope for these additions

- Exact titles, journal names, citation years and DOIs were checked against the linked official publisher pages on 2026-10-09. These are discovery records only; no whole-paper conversion, runnable task, robot-feasibility validation, safety qualification or reproduction claim is added
- **Bubble impact:** The paper reports physical air-bubble interactions with a quartz surface in glycerol-water solutions, recorded by high-speed imaging. Numerical sweeps and mechanical-model predictions remain separate from measured trajectories and geometry; the regime map is not wholly experimental. Main text and caption assignments for one experimental example disagree, and some main-to-SI section references do not match SI headings. These inconsistencies remain unresolved; no operating value or success threshold is adopted
- **Elastocapillarity:** The paper reports diffraction measurements and optical observations of facing hydrophobic laminae in water. Proposed task fit is limited to prepared-lamina observations: tip separation, air-bridge changes and repeat-measurement records. The capillarity model and interpretation of prior ion-channel literature are separate evidence, not new biological experiments. Negative compressibility concerns the solid component, not the combined solid-liquid system. A diffraction-capillary description and lamina pressure notation are internally inconsistent; neither is resolved here
- Task suitability is an authoring inference limited to supplied, qualified inputs and apparatus. Sample identity, instrument scheduling, image provenance and cycle histories could support future task design. Fluid/surface preparation, fabrication, sharp tools, gas-delivery setup, pressure/vacuum operation, synchrotron work and biological work are excluded from the proposed robot scope. Enclosure, liquid/electrical separation, fixtures, illumination and apparatus controls require independent qualification; no protocol or operating parameters are supplied
- **Access and reading:** Both main HTML sources were readable with cookie-error markers; clean live-browser access is not established. The bubble-impact SI PDF yielded selected experimental and validation text, but not a complete supplement audit. Its first selected-page rendering returned no viewable image to the reviewer; pixel inspection remains unverified, with no repeat. The elastocapillarity SI failed on its first actual file fetch and remains unread and resource-held, with no retry or alternate route. Main PDFs, figure pixels, movies and source data remain unreviewed; complete main-plus-SI review remains outstanding
- DOIs and normalized titles were checked against the 138 existing candidates, README and 114 task-source records whose blobs match a fresh remote tree; issue and PR searches found no matching source. Recovered exclusions were checked and known holds preserved. Incomplete historical exclusion recovery prevents exhaustive historical-screening claims
- The bubble-impact publisher page displays **CC BY-NC-ND 4.0**; the elastocapillarity page displays **CC BY 4.0**, with third-party credit-line exceptions. Only citations and independent factual discovery notes are included. No publisher prose, visuals or datasets are redistributed, and the repository license does not establish rights to adapt source material

Equal average velocities can conceal different trajectories; zero net transport need not mean rest. Chain position uses its terminal particles, whereas kink position is density-derived. These are different observables.
- Flexible/stiff-chain comparisons and repeated drive conditions provide preliminary control structure. Repeats do not establish independent sample preparations. The authors leave hydrodynamic coupling unresolved; the engineered pinned-chain kink does not directly resolve the faster density wave in a moving short chain.
- Suitability is an inference restricted to prepared sealed cells and qualified enclosed optical-trap, imaging, stage and magnetic-field services. Solvent handling, cell fabrication, exposed-beam alignment, source commissioning and biological work are excluded. Optical/interlock safety, magnet restraint, sample custody and robot interfaces require qualification; no procedures, settings, conversion or assets are supplied.
- Main HTML retained a cookie-error marker. No scientific SI link or supplementary-text reference appeared on the inspected publisher page; absence elsewhere is unverified. The cited setup/calibration source was not opened. Complete review, main PDF, image-based equations, figure pixels, raw trajectories and external references remain outstanding.
- DOI/title checks covered 169 prior candidates, README, 114 unchanged task-source records, issues/PRs and recovered exclusions/holds. Historical recovery remains incomplete. No failed or restricted source was retried or reached elsewhere. CC BY 4.0 has component-specific exceptions; only citation and independent discovery notes are included.

- Future scope requires qualified prepared specimens, enclosed/interlocked optics and vacuum systems, protected detectors and validated robot interfaces. Aerosol loading, cleaning, exposed beams and unqualified instrument operation are excluded.

## Additional candidate: programmable thermal-pulse metrology (2026-10-11)

One **CANDIDATE** is added after selected official main-text and scientific-SI screening. Reviewed-design and task-package counts are unchanged.

| Paper and official source | Journal / year | DOI | Preliminary task suitability | Source access checked |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Electronically driven soliton-like thermal pulses](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-026-72201-5) | Nature Communications / 2026 | 10.1038/s41467-026-72201-5 | Finished-platform condition registration and synchronized sensor/infrared measurements suggest a thermal-transport comparison task | Selected main HTML + scientific SI text; cookie marker; CC BY-NC-ND 4.0 |

### Evidence and limits

- The reported experiment uses ten copper sites with individually controlled thermoelectric couplers, temperature sensors and infrared imaging. Different boundary conditions produce localized pulses that stop at an edge or circulate while gradually decaying. These are finite-lived soliton-like responses; the paper distinguishes them from strict theoretical solitons
- Scientific SI Note 1 describes coupler and sensor calibration and error sources. Notes 4–5 describe theoretical multidimensional extensions and numerical propagation-distance sweeps; these do not establish a larger experimental apparatus
- Task suitability is an inference restricted to a future qualified finished platform. Electrical, contact-temperature, control-stability and output limits require separate review. No operating recipe, fabrication, execution or robot-feasibility claim is supplied
- Selected HTML and extracted text from the 32-page scientific SI were read. Main HTML returned usable content with a cookie-error query marker, which was not repaired or bypassed. Figure pixels, rendered equations, remaining SI sections, movies, Source Data and peer-review files were not reviewed
- Publisher licence: CC BY-NC-ND 4.0, subject to third-party exceptions. This is a citation/discovery record, not permission to share adaptations or redistribute source components
- Exact DOI/title checks passed the available catalog, README, task-source inventory and recursive restriction ledger. Historical inventory recovery remains incomplete, so exhaustive historical uniqueness is not claimed

## Additional candidate: nonlinear RF waveguide metrology (2026-10-12)

One additional **CANDIDATE** has selected main-text and scientific-supplement screening. Existing candidates and reviewed-design counts are unchanged.

| Paper and official source | Journal / year | DOI | Preliminary task suitability | Source access checked |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [Dynamic switching from coherent perfect absorption to parametric amplification in a nonlinear spoof plasmonic waveguide](https://www.nature.com/articles/s41467-024-47191-x) | Nature Communications / 2024 | 10.1038/s41467-024-47191-x | Prepared-waveguide exchange, instrument synchronization and phase/frequency comparisons suggest a staged RF configuration-and-measurement task | Official main HTML and 14-page scientific SI accessible through cloud Chrome; selected text screened; OA |

### Screening scope and limits

- Exact title, journal, year and DOI were verified on the publisher page. Selected experimental Results and Measurement Methods were screened alongside selected [scientific SI](https://media.springernature.com/original/springer-static/esm/art%3A10.1038%2Fs41467-024-47191-x/MediaObjects/41467_2024_47191_MOESM1_ESM.pdf) Notes 2–5. The title-matched technical supplement was distinguished from the separate peer-review file
- Potential suitability is limited to prepared devices and qualified RF metrology. Device identification, cable/connector custody, synchronized instrument settings and comparison records suggest long-horizon structure. This is an inference; fabrication, soldering, increased-power experiments and hardware commissioning are outside the proposed branch
- Different waveguide lengths are experimental design conditions. Unit-cell counts, phase points and repeated instrument readings do not establish independent specimen replication. Independent-device counts and repeat-trial totals remain unverified
- Measured gain comparisons remain separate from simulated scattering, calculated dispersion, calculated linewidth trends and length optimization. The effective nonlinear-parameter product is inferred from measurements. Conditional undepleted-pump assumptions, finite phase-setting precision and measured departures from the model remain explicit. In the measured reversed-frequency branch (SI Note 5/Fig. 7), coherent perfect absorption is limited to specific signal powers and phase tuning did not reach perfect transmission
- Main prose differs about which input arm contains the phase shifter; selected main/SI pump values also differ slightly. Neither is silently reconciled into an operating instruction
- Review was selective text screening, supplemented by independent inspection of SI page 14 pixels; mathematical extraction fidelity remains incomplete. Main figures, other SI pixels, raw data, code and external references were not reviewed. Neither source was exhaustively read; no new source access failure or retry occurred
- The page displays CC BY 4.0, subject to component credit lines. Known-identity/hold checks passed; the historical exclusion inventory remains incomplete. This adds a citation only, with no procedures, achievement targets or runnable task

- The publisher displays CC BY 4.0. This entry contains a citation and original suitability language; no source PDF, figure, code, dataset or operating procedure is redistributed.
- Exact identity checks found no match in the fresh catalog, README, nested task material, known prior restrictions or repository issue/PR search. The inherited historical-record gap remains unresolved.
